# Artifact runtime capabilities

A published Artifact page can declare **runtime capabilities** — abilities the claude.ai viewer grants the page at open time — by passing `capabilities: {name: config}` to the Artifact tool. The control plane is the authority on valid names and config shapes. Declaration gestures: **omitting** `capabilities` on a redeploy carries the stored declaration forward unchanged (and preserves the artifact's stored contract pin); an **empty object** `{}` is the explicit clear-all; a **non-empty object** is a full-set declaration (anything stored but not restated is revoked). Moving a republished artifact's runtime version is a deliberate gesture — pass `contract: 'latest'` to upgrade, or a specific version to pin or roll back — never a side effect of editing.

**A page that republishes itself** through the `artifact` capability sends its whole document in exactly the shape the Artifact tool publishes, so a later publish from the tool recognizes and replaces the skeleton instead of nesting it: `<!doctype html><html><head><meta charset=utf8><meta name=viewport content="width=device-width,initial-scale=1,viewport-fit=cover"><style>` the same small reset the tool's description names `</style></head><body>` — no whitespace between those tags and nothing else in the head — then the page content exactly as it was written for the tool (its `<title>` and `<style>` first, inside the body, regenerated from the page's state), then `</body></html>`.

**Available capabilities:** `artifact`, `assets`, `comments`, `db`, `downloads`, `files`, `mcp`, `room`, `sample`, `self`, `user` — the complete set of capability names you may declare; built in on every page, called without declaring (never pass these in `capabilities`): `permissions`. Anything not listed is unavailable to this user.

> Tool spelling in this session: the `Artifact` tool's `action: "read_db"` / `"write_db"` with a `db_op` are the `ArtifactData` tool, whose `action` is that `db_op` ("get", "list", "query", "set", "update", "str_replace", "delete", "batch") with the other fields unchanged — load it with ToolSearch when you first need it. Read the steps below with that substitution.

Runtime contract 0.2.75


Capability namespaces live behind `claude.use(name)`: `const db = await claude.use("db")` resolves the capability's namespace, or `null` when this view cannot run it (not served, not granted, or failed to load — indistinguishable by design). Branch on `null` and design for absence. `window.claude` carries only `use`: no `window.claude.db`, `.room`, or `.artifact` member is ever promised, so never read one — render the page without them and light features up when the promise resolves (later, never within your script's first run, and unordered with DOMContentLoaded; `null` after 10 s when no viewer answers). The resolved namespace is frozen and platform-owned: call its functions and keep the reference; never assign to it, `defineProperty` on it, or replace a member (wrap it for your own helpers). Permission stays on the calls: a consent prompt, rate limit, or policy refusal arrives on the first call, never from `use()`. Awaiting `use("db")` again is free (memoized); an unknown name resolves `null`.


--- capability: artifact ---

Use `artifact` for pages that should remember what people do with them: polls, sign-up sheets, checklists, trackers, boards — the page is the record; data kept server-side, or seeded or read back by Claude, is `db`. Declare `capabilities: {artifact: {}}`; `const artifact = await claude.use("artifact")`, then `await artifact.publish(html)` saves `html` (a complete document, doctype first) as the new version, and every open view, this one included, reloads to it. Nothing a viewer types, ticks or drags is kept unless the page publishes it. So embed the shared state as data in the HTML you publish and render the page from it; when an interaction completes, update the state, regenerate the document and publish it — never serialize the live DOM; batch rapid edits into one publish; publish only after a viewer acts, never on load. `conflict` is routine (every view reloads to the winner, dropping this edit): no retry. For read-only viewers publish rejects `not_granted`/`not_writer` — render a read-only view.


--- capability: assets ---

`assets` stores uploaded assets for this artifact: `const assets = await claude.use("assets")`; `await assets.upload(blob)` (image, SVG, video, PDF, font, CSS/JS, or CSV/Markdown/JSON/text data; 20 MiB cap, CSS/JS 16 MiB, SVG 2 MiB and sanitized on upload) resolves `{id, url, sizeBytes, contentType}`; `assets.list()` resolves `{assets, usage}` (storage meter, orphan pruning); `assets.delete(id)` removes one for good: only on a deliberate user action, updating the `db` rows that held the id. Declare `capabilities: {assets: {}}`; a declaring page is organization-internal (never public). Writer-only: a reader view gets `null` from `use("assets")`; hide asset UI on `null` and handle rejection codes. Store the `id` in `db` rows as the durable pointer and index; use the returned `url` as-is as an `<img>`/`<video>`/`<a>` source (SVG: `<img>` or CSS only); a stored id serves at `"/_blob/" + id` in every view. Quota: per artifact (`usage`). The type definitions are authoritative for accepted types and error codes.


--- capability: comments ---

`comments` wires a page's own commenting UI to the artifact's shared comment store: `await claude.use("comments")` (`null`: unavailable). The declaration picks the grant: `capabilities: {comments: {"composer_only": true}}` grants only `openComposer({element}|{range})` (opens the shell's composer like a comment-mode click; prefer it for discoverable entry points) and `anchorFor`, no consent asked. The full form `{comments: {}}` adds write verbs acting as the viewer under consent; public-link visitors and email invitees get `null`. `"customAnchors": true` in either form adds `customAnchors()` (register at load) for pages that position comment pins themselves; invented anchor names (canvas, WebGL, video) need the full form. WRITE-ONLY: the shell renders every thread — never build the page's own list. Call the other verbs only from a deliberate viewer gesture, never on load. Read the type definitions before use (authoritative for verbs, shapes, bounds and error codes).


--- capability: db ---

`db` keeps data outside the page: shared rows, per-person state, data Claude reads. Declare `capabilities:{db:{},user:{}}`; `const db = await claude.use("db")` (`null` if signed out). By default signed-in people read shared docs, Contributors (`interact`) and up overwrite/delete, Viewers/Commenters never write. `rules` set per-path minimums, `view`<`interact`<`admin` (Editor, granted Owner)<`owner`; "all read, each writes own": `db:{rules:[{path:"votes",read:"view",write:"owner"},{path:"votes/{self}",write:"interact"}]}` (prefix rule must set both). `data/users/<id>/` is private even from the owner; `<id>` = `await user.id()`. Even segment counts are docs (`db.doc("tasks/t1")`), odd are collections; per-person items: `db.collection("data/users/"+id).doc(k)`; get/set/update/delete, where/orderBy/limit, onSnapshot. Never hardcode seeds: `write_db` after publish; handle an empty db. Subscribe once per query, never in render; one write at a time per doc, only on change. No secrets; shared data is untrusted.


--- capability: downloads ---

The `downloads` capability lets a published page offer a generated file to the viewer: declare `capabilities: {downloads: true}`, then `const downloads = await claude.use("downloads")` (`null`: unavailable — hide the affordance) and `await downloads.save({filename, data})`. The viewer sees a confirmation and may decline — a save is never silent or guaranteed, so offer it on explicit viewer intent and handle rejection. The type

# Small Talk — ChatGPT artwork pack (v2, HD, board-exact)

## Why the current art looks soft
- The women came from one ChatGPT image with all seven side by side, so each woman is only about 300 px wide. Phones show her at 400–600 px, so she gets stretched.
- The nails and the finger are about 200–300 px wide, but the hero shows them at 2–3 times that.
- Several board pieces were never made as separate files (receipt hand, rotary phone, sketchbook, safety-pin fabric, cards hand), so the build uses stand-ins with hard fade edges.
- Files sent through WhatsApp are compressed again.

## The four HD rules (follow every time)
1. **One subject per image.** Never ask for several sets in one picture. The subject should fill the frame.
2. **Largest size.** Ask for the tall size (1024 × 1536) for people and nails, square (1024 × 1024) for product photos, wide (1536 × 1024) for props.
3. **Download the original.** Open the image in ChatGPT, use the download button, save the PNG. Never screenshot, never pass it through WhatsApp.
4. **Transparent background** for everything marked "cutout". If ChatGPT gives a background anyway, reply: "Same image, transparent background, change nothing else. If you can't, use a flat pure #00FF00 background." If it still fails, accept a flat plain background in pure green #00FF00 (Green Flag: magenta #FF00FF; Wild Card: cyan #00FFFF); we cut it out. Never use the set colour as the background for a cutout.

- **One ChatGPT chat per set** (se

# 00 — Small Talk copy register: index, gates, decisions

Copy register v1.3 · 9 Oct 2026 · written by the copy session (sole writer of shopper-visible words). The copy session writes nothing to Shopify. The orchestrator installed the owner-approved shared rows marked “installed”. Shared records (products, metaobjects, articles, pages, menus, SEO fields, policies) need separate review before installation. Theme locale and section settings are theme-local.

# Small Talk: Terms of service (draft for the owner to paste)

Where to paste: Shopify admin → Settings → Policies → Terms of service. Once it is saved, the footer “Terms” link works.
This is a plain-language draft for a test store, not legal advice. Before real sales:
- Swap “Small Talk” for your registered business name if you have one.
- Check that it matches how you actually operate.
- Have a lawyer review it if you can.

---

## Terms of service

Last updated: 9 October 2026

### 1. Who we are
This website is run by Small Talk (“we”, “us”). By using the site or placing an order, you agree to these terms. If you don’t agree, please don’t use the site.

### 2. Our products
We sell press-on nail sets. Product colours on screen can look slightly different from the real thing. Campaign illustrations on the site are artwork, not photos of the product. Please follow the instructions that come with your kit and on our Nail Guide.

### 3. Prices and payment
Prices are in Indian rupees (INR) and include GST. Any shipping charge is shown at checkout before you pay. We can change prices at any time, but not for an order you have already placed. If a price is clearly wrong because of an error, we may cancel the order and refund you in full.

### 4. Orders
Placing an order is an offer to buy. Your order is accepted when we dispatch it. We may decline or cancel an order (for example, if an item is out of stock), and if we do, we refund you in full.

  - Posting ramps from 1 a day in weeks 1–2 to 2 a day in weeks 3–4, then 2–3 a day once the weekly batch keeps quality up.
  - It has a weekly-numbers table for each stage and a weekly batch-day routine linked to the carousel and AI-video sections.

P4 · LATER (month 2+, once there's traffic and data):

Addition to the priority ranking I just sent. The owner's words: "see what task is dependent on what task. So I don't want you to give a high priority task, the dependent previous tasks of which have not been done yet."
Add DEPENDENCIES and make both the list and "Do this next" respect them.
Rules:
1. Each task gets needs: [task ids]. A task is READY only when every task it needs is ticked.
2. "Do this next" = the highest-priority READY unticked task. Never show a blocked task there.
3. In the priority list, a blocked task stays in its tier but shows a ⏳ "waiting on: <prerequisite title>" line, linked to that card, and sits visually muted. Don't use 🔒; that already means "only you".
4. If a higher-tier task needs a lower-tier one, list the prerequisite immediately before it, with a "needed first for <task>" note, so nothing ever ranks above what it depends on (topological order within the priority order).
5. Time-based waits: store the date when a task is ticked. If a dependency needs time to pass (e.g. ads need 2–4 weeks of pixel data), show "ready from <date>". Before then it's blocked.
6. External waits that aren't Riya's tasks (e.g. "talks posting is open", "store is published") show as "waiting on: <event>" and clear when the matching task is ticked, or a status in "Our list" is set to done.

- creator/blog mentions ← store published + Instagram profile tune-up
- retargeting: audiences ← events check + 2–4 weeks since the events check was ticked (use 14 days minimum); ad copy ← carousel voice; first campaign ← audiences + ad copy + payments; weekly ad check ← first campaign

**What changed:**
- "Do this next" now shows the highest-priority task that is unticked and whose prerequisites are all ticked. A blocked task never appears there, and the three optional tasks are still skipped.
- In the priority list, a blocked task stays in its tier but is muted and shows ⏳ instead of "← next". Under it, an amber "⏳ waiting on: <title>" line links to each prerequisite's card; outside events show by name.
- The same waiting line sits at the top of the task's card, and a small "⏳ waiting" chip appears on its bar. All of this updates live as tasks are ticked.
- **Time waits:** use the date stored when the prerequisite was ticked. Before then they show "⏳ ready from 24 Oct (needs time for data to build up)". If the prerequisite isn't ticked yet, the line adds "(then 14 days after it's ticked)".

- **ig-setup:** has no prerequisites, because the carousel voice is already inside it. ig-ideas needs ig-setup.
- **a-camp:** needs ig-setup for the voice, a-start and pay. Ad copy is part of a-camp.
- **e-wa:** has no prerequisites, because its consent rules are inside the task.
- **v-example:** has no prerequisites; it's reading only.
- **m-app:** needs only the portfolio and the professional account, as in your map. I removed its old step "do this after the store is published". The Meta pixel is added through the app, not the theme, so it works on whichever theme is live.
